MARKET DIRECTION: DOWN — NIFTY in a downtrend since Wed, 2 Sept 2026. No new buys (no futures, no calls): puts instead — 357 on 37 names (4 indices, 33 stocks).
NIFTY closed at 22,421.95 on Thu, 1 Oct 2026; its 20-day average 23,221.49, its 50-day 23,870.1, its 200-day 24,355.76. A close under the 20-day average, with the 20-day under the 50-day, is what the desk’s trend reader (market-regime.js) calls a downtrend. It is read from completed daily closes, so it does not change during the day. For puts these figures are the OPTIMISTIC end. Each put is repriced with Black-76 at its own implied volatility, held where it is — but a put loses on the days the market rises, and on those days implied volatility usually falls, which takes still more off a put (India VIX moved −7% on Tue, 3 Feb 2026). The real loss on such a day can be larger than shown.

Hit rate counts only trades that filled and then finished. IF RANDOM is how often the target would come before the stop if prices moved at random — each trade's break-even, before charges. Only a hit rate above it, over enough separate bets, is evidence of an edge. The touch odds the desk showed ignore the stop, so they always run higher. Futures returns are GROSS per unit; options are NET of every charge per lot. ALL has no average return (futures move about 1.5%, options about 50%), and its money is options net a lot plus futures at 20 units, gross.

HOW THIS IS SCORED. Every price this page freezes is bought on paper at the moment it is shown. Each build then checks the market, using the day’s high and low so a move between two builds is not missed. Every ambiguity counts against the record: a target and a stop in the same window is a stop; a target in the same window as the fill is not counted; a gap through the stop exits at the open; a fill is always at the limit, never better; a price from before the level was shown can never fill it. Unfilled day orders die at 15:30. Futures are held at most 20 sessions; options until expiry. Stored in the private repository under docs/ledger/, committed on every build, and copied to the Google Sheet.
WHERE THESE PRICES COME FROM. All prices are from the 2026-10-01 settlement file. The market was closed when this was built, so nothing here is executable right now. Premiums and the futures prices they are measured against both come from that same settlement file, so they describe the same moment. Checked against NSE's own site at 2026-10-02 15:54 IST: 12 of 12 sampled contracts are the same contract on both (the same previous close), last prices within 3.73%. Broker login OK from the build server at 2026-10-02 15:54 IST — a NIFTY quote came back. F&O ban for 2026-10-05: AMBUJACEM, BANDHANBNK, SAIL — left out entirely, because NSE allows only exits in a banned stock. This page is a snapshot, rebuilt on a schedule — it is not a live feed. Check the price on your broker before placing anything. Net figures are after brokerage, STT at 0.15%, exchange, stamp and GST; the spread is extra and is only known where a live book was seen.
WHAT THIS DOES NOT ESTABLISH. That any of it makes money. The paper ledger has 772 resolved trade(s) so far — far too few for a measured hit rate — and every test fixed in advance so far, on data never used to build the rule, found no edge: the desk's own ranking (final year t = 0.75), 12-1 momentum, time-series momentum, low volatility, a 10-month market trend filter, and the NIFTY trend reader that now chooses between buys and puts (selling NIFTY in its downtrends: final year t = 0.21) — all six FAIL. The ranking is built on things that were measured, which is not the same as an edge that was proven. Size accordingly.
